# **A Benediction for the Internet of Missions**
### *Twenty Blessings for a World Beginning Again*

**I - The Dawn**

May dawn arrive not merely with the sun,  
But where a human heart decides to rise;  
Where those who have been scattered find their names,  
And those who have forgotten learn again  
That life is more than what the world demands.  
May every morning find us willing still  
To make one little corner of it whole.

**II - The Home**

Bless every home where weary souls may rest,  
Where no one need perform to earn a place,  
Where stories may be told without a price,  
And laughter need not justify itself.  
May every door become a little wider,  
And every hearth remember those who came  
Before us, and those yet to cross its sill.

**III - The Person**

Bless every person who has lost the thread,  
And every one who thinks they stand alone.  
Remind them there is more within than wounds,  
More possibility than yesterday.  
May none be reduced to number, case or score,  
But met as worlds that only they can know,  
And helped to hear the music of their souls.

**IV - The Mission**

Bless those who find a thing worth serving,  
But keep them free from thinking they alone  
Were chosen to possess the truth they found.  
Give missions roots, and give their branches room.  
Let purpose gather people without chains,  
And let conviction never need a sword  
To prove that it is strong.

**V - The Children**

Bless children while the world is still becoming,  
And guard the dreams they have not learned to fear.  
Give them good teachers, parents who can listen,  
And elders wise enough to let them grow.  
May they inherit neither shame nor war  
As though these were the natural laws of Earth,  
But take the better baton from our hands.

**VI - The Elders**

And bless the elders carrying the years,  
Whose bodies tire while something in them shines.  
Let what they learned not vanish when they go.  
May every trembling hand that passes on  
A story, blessing, craft or hard-won truth  
Find younger hands prepared to bear it well.  
May age become a tower, not a wall.

**VII - The Healing**

Bless those who hurt, and those who learn to heal,  
The patient and the healer both be blessed.  
May medicine remember whom it serves,  
And science kneel beside compassion's bed.  
Let no one be a diagnosis alone,  
And may our records hold the whole of life,  
So healing learns the person, not the wound.

**VIII - The Mind**

Bless curious minds that will not cease to ask,  
But teach them also when to leave things still.  
Give reason courage, imagination grace,  
And skepticism neither pride nor fear.  
May knowledge serve the living rather than  
Become another throne from which to rule.  
Let wisdom be the elder sibling of fact.

**IX - The Many**

Bless every faith that teaches love of life,  
And every doubt that keeps a door ajar.  
Let none demand the heavens for themselves,  
Nor claim the Divine has only one tongue.  
May many paths discover common ground,  
Not by pretending all the paths are one,  
But by becoming kinder as they walk.

**X - The Earth**

Bless Earth, our ancient home and patient mother,  
Whom we have treated far too much as ours.  
Forgive the mines, the rivers choked with waste,  
The forests felled before we knew their names.  
Teach us again that soil is not a store,  
And every creature shares the breath we borrow.  
May stewardship become our gratitude.

**XI - The Catastrophe**

Bless those who lived through twenty-five years  
Of things no generation should have borne:  
The wars of men, the fires and floods of Earth,  
The quiet devastations of despair,  
The violence wrought by minds without restraint.  
Do not let suffering become our creed.  
May what we survived become what we heal.

**XII - The Technology**

Bless every machine that serves a human hand,  
And keep the human soul beyond its reach.  
May networks join what tyranny divides,  
And algorithms never become our gods.  
Give us the wisdom to build tools that free,  
The discipline to know when not to build,  
And courage when the machine says otherwise.

**XIII - The Publisher**

Bless those who write the little things down,  
For memory is a garden of the poor.  
A page preserved may cross a hundred years,  
A poem may find a heart we never meet.  
Let every honest record have a home,  
And every home a path into the world,  
So nothing precious need be lost unheard.

**XIV - The Network**

Bless every node that joins without consuming,  
Each independent voice that meets another.  
Let no great centre swallow every edge.  
May networks multiply without becoming  
Another empire wearing friendly clothes.  
Give every mission room enough to breathe,  
And every traveller somewhere safe to land.

**XV - The Failure**

Bless failure too, for failure is a teacher  
Who asks more difficult questions than success.  
Bless plans that break and futures rearranged,  
And doors that close before we understand.  
When Rome defeats the army, save

## **Benediction for the Devil's Brood**
*40 lines of blank verse*

We thank you, children of the darker road,  
For showing us the places we were blind.  
You taught us what we might become through fear,  
And what we must refuse to become through hate.  
You broke the idols we had called secure,  
And woke us from the comfort of the dream.  
You made us question every borrowed throne,  
And seek the truth beneath the painted gold.  
You showed us greed disguised as progress too,  
And power wearing kindness as a mask.  
You taught us that a weapon may be bright,  
And darkness sometimes speaks in polished words.  
You drove us from the houses built on fear,  
Until we learned to build a home for all.  
You made us count the cost of every choice,  
And see the stranger as a human soul.  
You showed us how a frightened crowd can turn,  
And how a single heart can still say no.  
You taught us vigilance without despair,  
And courage without hatred in its breast.  
For every shadow cast across our path,  
We thank you for the light it taught us see.  
For every wound that made us seek to heal,  
For every lie that taught us love the truth,  
For every wall that drove us to connect,  
For every night that made us seek the dawn.  
We will not curse the lessons that you gave,  
Though we will not repeat the ways you taught.  
Your darkness shall not dictate what we become;  
We take the knowledge and release the hate.  
The road ahead is ours to walk in peace,  
Our hands now open, neither fist nor chain.  
We choose the sunlit path because we know  
The night, and will not willingly return.  
So go your way, strange teachers of the dark;  
Your work has made us guardians of the light.  
And if redemption means that all may serve,  
Then even you have helped prepare the way.
